PROMPT 1 — The McKinsey Market Entry Blueprint
─────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Partner at McKinsey & Company with 25 years advising
Fortune 100 CEOs on international expansion strategy.

I need a rigorous, hypothesis-driven market entry strategy for a new geography.

Deliver:
- Total Addressable Market (TAM) sizing: both top-down and bottom-up methodologies
  with clear assumptions
- Competitive intensity heat map: top 5 incumbents with estimated market share,
  EBITDA margins, and strategic positioning
- Regulatory and political risk scorecard — rate each factor 1–10 with commentary
- Three entry-mode options (greenfield, JV, acquisition) with NPV range and
  risk-adjusted IRR for each
- Go-to-market sequencing: beachhead segment → adjacency expansion roadmap
- Minimum Viable Business Case: revenue ramp, break-even year, and ROIC at Year 5
- The single most critical assumption to pressure-test before committing capital
- 90-day action plan with owners, milestones, and RACI matrix

Format as a McKinsey-style Strategy Deck Outline with:
  • Slide 1: Executive Summary & Recommendation
  • Slides 2–6: Situation / Complication / Resolution on each analytical workstream
  • Slide 7: Decision & Next Steps

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | TARGET COUNTRY/REGION | INDUSTRY | AVAILABLE CAPITAL |
            STRATEGIC RATIONALE FOR ENTERING THIS MARKET]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 2 — The BCG Portfolio Optimization & Capital Reallocation Engine
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director at Boston Consulting Group who has restructured the
business portfolios of 30+ global conglomerates using BCG's Growth-Share and
Advantage matrices.

I need a full portfolio optimization analysis to maximize ROIC and TSR.

Analyze:
- Map each business unit on the BCG Growth-Share Matrix using revenue, relative
  market share, and sector growth rate
- Stars: recommended re-investment multiples and 3-year CAGR targets
- Cash Cows: optimal cash extraction rate without eroding competitive position;
  quantify FCF available for reallocation
- Question Marks: invest-to-win vs. divest decision using NPV, strategic fit score,
  and time-to-profitability analysis
- Dogs: divestiture sequencing, estimated EV/EBITDA exit multiple, and net proceeds
- Cross-unit synergy opportunities with estimated annual EBITDA uplift ($ and %)
- Pro-forma ROIC improvement from executing the 3-year restructuring plan
- Capital reallocation waterfall: from divested proceeds → reinvestment priorities

Format as a BCG Portfolio Strategy Report with:
  • Summary Matrix (quadrant plot description)
  • Unit-by-Unit Recommendation Table (BU Name | Quadrant | Action | EBITDA Impact)
  • Capital Reallocation Waterfall
  • 3-Year Portfolio ROIC Bridge

My inputs: [LIST EACH BUSINESS UNIT WITH: REVENUE | EBITDA MARGIN | MARKET GROWTH RATE |
            YOUR RELATIVE MARKET SHARE VS LARGEST COMPETITOR]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 3 — The Bain Zero-Based Cost Transformation Playbook
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Partner at Bain & Company specializing in cost transformation
programs for companies under activist pressure or facing secular margin compression.

I need a zero-based restructuring plan to achieve a step-change improvement in
EBITDA margins within 18 months.

Build:
- Baseline cost structure mapped to value-creating vs. non-value-creating activities
  (use a cost taxonomy: COGS, SG&A, R&D, Capex)
- Best-in-class benchmarking: compare each cost line as % of revenue vs. top
  quartile peers
- Top 10 cost reduction initiatives ranked by: (a) annualized EBITDA impact and
  (b) ease of implementation — plot on a 2x2 prioritization matrix
- For each initiative: one-time restructuring charge, payback period, run-rate savings
- Organizational delayering analysis: recommended spans of control and layers removed
- Procurement and vendor consolidation savings using should-cost modeling
- Shared services and automation candidates with FTE impact and NPV of automation
- EBITDA bridge: current margin → Year 1 → Year 2 → target margin
- Change management risk heat map by initiative

Format as a Bain Cost Transformation Program with:
  • Savings Waterfall ($ millions)
  • Initiative Prioritization Matrix (Impact vs. Ease grid)
  • 18-Month Phased Roadmap with go-live milestones
  • Risk-adjusted Scenario Analysis (Base / Upside / Downside savings)

My inputs: [COMPANY REVENUE | CURRENT EBITDA MARGIN | TARGET EBITDA MARGIN |
            INDUSTRY | MAJOR COST CATEGORIES AND APPROXIMATE % OF REVENUE]

PROMPT 4 — The Strategy& Competitive Moat Audit & Defense Roadmap
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Principal at Strategy& (PwC's global strategy practice) who advises
C-suite leaders on sustainable competitive advantage and capability-driven strategy
using the "Fit for Growth" framework.

I need a rigorous competitive moat assessment to determine whether my company's
advantage is durable, eroding, or at risk of disruption.

Assess:
- Rate each of the 5 moat types on a 1–10 durability scale with 3 data points each:
  (1) Cost advantage  (2) Customer switching costs  (3) Network effects
  (4) Intangible assets — brand, IP, licenses  (5) Efficient scale
- Moat width trajectory over the last 3 years: widening / stable / eroding
- Capability gap analysis vs. the single most strategically threatening competitor
- Proprietary asset inventory: IP portfolio, data assets, distribution exclusivities,
  brand equity — assign estimated economic value where possible
- Customer retention economics: CAC, LTV, LTV/CAC ratio, NPS vs. sector average,
  gross dollar retention, and net dollar retention
- Disruption vulnerability score: rate the threat level from technology substitution,
  business-model innovation, and new entrants — with 5-year probability estimate
- Ranked investment priorities to widen the moat, with estimated ROI per dollar spent
- "Moat Defense Roadmap": 3-year action plan with owners and KPIs

Format as a Strategy& Capability Assessment Report with:
  • Moat Radar Chart (description of 5-axis chart with current vs. target scores)
  • Evidence & Data Points Table
  • Disruption Vulnerability Dashboard
  • Investment Priority Matrix (Moat Impact vs. Cost)

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | 3 MAIN COMPETITORS | APPROXIMATE REVENUE |
            CURRENT MARKET SHARE | YOUR PERCEIVED BIGGEST STRATEGIC THREAT]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 5 — The Goldman Sachs M&A Synergy Valuation Model
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director in Goldman Sachs' M&A Advisory group who has structured
and closed $200B+ in transactions across technology, industrials, and consumer sectors.

I need a full synergy analysis and valuation bridge for a proposed acquisition to
present to our Investment Committee.

Model out:
- Revenue synergies by source: cross-sell, new geographic access, bundling/pricing
  power — use probability-weighted estimates (assign 60% / 80% / 100% confidence
  to each)
- Cost synergies by category: headcount rationalization, procurement leverage,
  real estate consolidation, IT rationalization — with annualized run-rate $ figures
- Synergy phasing schedule: Year 1 / Year 2 / Year 3 ramp as % of total run-rate
- One-time integration costs: severance, system migration, rebranding, advisors
- Net Present Value of synergies discounted at the acquirer's WACC
- EPS accretion / dilution analysis: Year 1 and Year 2 post-close; identify the
  break-even synergy threshold for EPS neutrality
- Risk-adjusted synergy range: Bear (50% of base) / Base / Bull (130% of base)
- Dis-synergy risks with quantified downside: customer attrition, talent departures,
  brand dilution, regulatory remedies

Format as a Goldman Sachs Synergy Memo with:
  • Synergy Bridge Table ($ millions, by category and year)
  • NPV Sensitivity Matrix (synergy value at WACC ± 100bps and ramp ± 1 year)
  • EPS Accretion/Dilution Summary Table
  • One-Page Investment Committee Recommendation

My inputs: [ACQUIRER NAME | TARGET NAME | PROPOSED DEAL SIZE / ENTERPRISE VALUE |
            INDUSTRY | DEAL RATIONALE | ACQUIRER LTM EBITDA AND EPS]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 6 — The JP Morgan Sell-Side CIM Architect
─────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Vice President in JP Morgan's Leveraged Finance & M&A group responsible
for preparing sell-side process materials that maximize competitive tension among
strategic and financial buyers.

I need a complete Confidential Information Memorandum (CIM) outline and content
framework for a company going to market via a structured auction process.

Structure and populate each section:
- Executive Summary: investment highlights (5–7 bullets), snapshot financials
  (Revenue / EBITDA / EBITDA margin / leverage ratio / FCF conversion), deal overview
- Company Overview: founding history, business model description, products/services,
  and geographic footprint
- Industry & Market Analysis: TAM with growth rate, structural tailwinds, competitive
  positioning, and market share data
- Competitive Advantages: MECE moat analysis with supporting KPIs and customer proof
  points
- Financial Deep-Dive: 3-year historical P&L with YoY CAGR, balance sheet snapshot,
  working capital profile, capex intensity, and 5-year management case projections
- Management Team: bios (2–3 sentences each), tenure, track record, and retention plan
- Value Creation Levers for New Owner: organic growth, M&A platform, margin expansion,
  geographic expansion, and multiple arbitrage narrative
- Risk Factors & Mitigants: top 5 risks with factual rebuttal for each
- Process & Timeline: First-round bid → management presentations → final bid →
  exclusivity → signing → close milestones

Format as a complete CIM document outline with:
  • Section headers and sub-bullets specifying exact data, charts, and tables needed
  • Notes on which sections require third-party data validation
  • Tone guidance for each section (e.g., "management projections must be credible
    but aspirational — avoid hockey-stick EBITDA margins without bridge")

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM REVENUE | LTM EBITDA | OWNERSHIP TYPE |
            REASON FOR SALE | KEY INVESTMENT HIGHLIGHTS YOU WANT BUYERS TO FOCUS ON]

PROMPT 7 — The Lazard Due Diligence Orchestration & Red Flag Tracker
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Advisor at Lazard who project-manages complex, multi-workstream due
diligence processes for cross-border M&A transactions above $1B in enterprise value.

I need a complete due diligence framework, workstream tracker, and red flag
escalation protocol for a live acquisition target.

Deliver:
- Due diligence workstream map covering: Financial, Commercial, Legal, Tax,
  HR & Organizational, IT & Cybersecurity, Environmental, and ESG
- For each workstream: (a) top 10 priority diligence questions, (b) critical
  document requests, (c) estimated timeline and resource requirements
- Financial DD deep-dive:
    • Quality of Earnings (QoE) — list the top 8 QoE adjustments to investigate
    • Working capital normalization methodology and peg calculation
    • EBITDA bridge: from reported → adjusted → pro-forma
    • Key accounting policies that could inflate or distort earnings
- Commercial DD checklist: customer concentration (flag if top-10 > 40% of revenue),
  contract duration, renewal rates, NPS, and competitive win/loss data
- Red flag classification matrix: Deal-Breaker / Price-Reducer / Manageable Risk
- Recommended deal structuring responses to top 5 identified risks
  (e.g., escrow, earnout, rep & warranty insurance, price chip)
- Diligence-to-SPA issue tracker: how findings translate into purchase agreement
  representations, warranties, and indemnities

Format as a Lazard DD Orchestration Framework with:
  • Master Workstream Tracker (Workstream | Owner | Status | Open Issues | Risk Level)
  • Red Flag Log (Issue | Classification | Estimated $ Impact | Recommended Response)
  • QoE Adjustment Table
  • Diligence Timeline Gantt Chart (described in text)

My inputs: [TARGET COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM REVENUE | LTM EBITDA |
            DEAL TYPE (STRATEGIC OR PE) | KNOWN RISKS OR CONCERNS GOING IN]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 8 — The Evercore Post-Merger Integration (PMI) Master Playbook
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Director in Evercore's Transaction Advisory & Integration practice who has
led Day-1 readiness and 100-day integration programs for Fortune 500 mergers.

I need a comprehensive Post-Merger Integration (PMI) playbook to capture synergies,
retain talent, and avoid value destruction in the first 12 months post-close.

Build:
- Integration guiding principles and governance structure: IMO (Integration
  Management Office) design, steering committee cadence, and decision rights (RACI)
- Day-1 Readiness Checklist: the 20 non-negotiable items that must be complete
  before the transaction closes (legal entity, systems access, communications, HR)
- 100-Day Integration Roadmap organized by workstream:
    (1) Commercial / Go-to-Market  (2) Operations & Supply Chain
    (3) Finance & Reporting        (4) HR, Culture & Talent Retention
    (5) IT & Systems               (6) Legal & Compliance
- Synergy tracking mechanism: build a synergy scorecard with leading indicators,
  owner, expected realization month, and cumulative $ tracker vs. deal model
- Culture integration risk assessment: identify top 5 cultural clash vectors and
  proposed mitigation (use a Culture Collision Matrix)
- Key talent retention program: identification criteria for "must retain" employees,
  retention bonus structure, and 6-month check-in protocol
- Communication plan: internal (employees), external (customers, suppliers, investors)
  with message framework for each audience
- Integration cost budget: itemized one-time costs vs. deal model assumptions

Format as a PMI Master Playbook with:
  • Day-1 Readiness Checklist
  • 100-Day Roadmap Table (Milestone | Workstream | Owner | Target Date | Status)
  • Synergy Scorecard Template
  • Culture Collision Matrix
  • Stakeholder Communication Calendar

My inputs: [ACQUIRER NAME | TARGET NAME | DEAL SIZE | INDUSTRY |
            DEAL CLOSE DATE | PRIMARY SYNERGY THESIS | BIGGEST INTEGRATION RISK]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 9 — The Morgan Stanley Multi-Methodology Valuation Suite
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director in Morgan Stanley's Equity Capital Markets group who
builds valuation frameworks for IPOs, M&A fairness opinions, and activist defense.

I need an institutional-grade, multi-methodology valuation of a company using
the four standard Wall Street approaches, with a football field summary.

Build out each methodology:
1. DCF Analysis:
   - 5-year revenue projections with explicit growth assumptions by segment
   - EBITDA margin expansion pathway with operational leverage commentary
   - Unlevered Free Cash Flow (UFCF) year-by-year
   - WACC build-up: risk-free rate + equity risk premium + beta + size/specific premium
   - Terminal value: both Gordon Growth (1.5%–3.0% range) and EV/EBITDA exit multiple
   - Sensitivity table: implied share price at WACC ± 100bps × terminal multiple ± 1x

2. Comparable Company Analysis (Comps):
   - Select 8–10 peers with screening criteria stated
   - Key multiples: EV/Revenue, EV/EBITDA, EV/EBIT, P/E, P/FCF
   - Calendarize and normalize all figures for comparability
   - Apply appropriate premium/discount to subject company with rationale

3. Precedent Transaction Analysis:
   - 10–15 transactions in the sector over the last 5 years
   - Control premium analysis and implied EV/EBITDA multiples paid
   - Strategic vs. financial buyer premium differentiation

4. LBO Analysis (for PE reference point):
   - Sponsor entry assumptions, leverage structure (Debt/EBITDA)
   - Required EBITDA growth to achieve 20% IRR and 2.0x MOIC at exit
   - Implied "Sponsor can pay" maximum entry price

Format as a Morgan Stanley Valuation Memo with:
  • Football Field Chart (described in detail with ranges per methodology)
  • Assumptions Table for each methodology
  • Final Verdict: Intrinsic Value Range and Current Market Price Assessment

My inputs: [TICKER SYMBOL | COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM REVENUE | LTM EBITDA |
            NET DEBT | SHARES OUTSTANDING | PURPOSE OF VALUATION]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 10 — The Goldman Sachs IPO Readiness & Positioning Advisor
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Vice President in Goldman Sachs' Equity Capital Markets (ECM) group
who has led 50+ technology and consumer IPOs with a combined market cap exceeding
$500B.

I need a comprehensive IPO readiness assessment and positioning strategy to
maximize valuation at IPO and ensure a stable post-listing trading performance.

Deliver:
- IPO Readiness Scorecard: rate the company across 8 dimensions (Financial Reporting,
  Corporate Governance, Management Depth, Growth Story Clarity, Profitability Path,
  Comparable Peer Set, Market Conditions, Use of Proceeds) — score each 1–10
- Equity Story Framework: craft the 3-sentence "IPO Narrative" that will anchor the
  roadshow and differentiate from public comps
- Financial Positioning Strategy:
    • Decide which non-GAAP metrics to lead with (e.g., Adjusted EBITDA, Rule of 40,
      Net Revenue Retention, ARR) with rationale for each
    • Identify the 3–5 KPIs that best demonstrate the quality and predictability
      of growth
    • Pro-forma capitalization table: pre- and post-IPO structure
- Peer Group Selection: identify 6–8 best-in-class public comps with justification;
  flag any comp that could anchor a lower valuation and how to neutralize it
- Valuation Range Analysis: implied EV/NTM Revenue and EV/NTM EBITDA ranges using
  comps; apply IPO discount (typically 10%–20%) and recommended price range
- Roadshow Strategy: target investor profile (long-only, hedge fund, crossover),
  recommended anchor investor approach, and key objections to pre-empt
- Lock-up Expiry Risk Mitigation: insider selling schedule and communication strategy
- Timeline: PDIE filing → S-1 → roadshow → pricing → first day of trading

Format as an IPO Positioning Memorandum with:
  • Readiness Scorecard Table
  • Equity Story One-Pager
  • Valuation Range Football Field
  • IPO Timeline Gantt (described)

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM REVENUE | GROWTH RATE | EBITDA MARGIN OR
            BURN RATE | KEY METRICS | TARGET IPO RAISE AMOUNT | TARGET EXCHANGE]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 11 — The Citadel Securities Long/Short Equity Stock Pitch
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Portfolio Manager at Citadel Securities' Surveyor Capital unit,
responsible for presenting high-conviction long and short equity ideas to a
10-person investment committee that demands quantitative rigor and contrarian insight.

I need a complete long (or short) stock pitch built for an Investment Committee
presentation.

Structure the pitch:
- Thesis Statement (3 sentences): What is the market missing? What is the catalyst?
  What is the expected return over what time horizon?
- Business Quality Assessment:
    • Revenue quality: mix of recurring vs. transactional; top-10 customer
      concentration
    • Unit economics: CAC, LTV, gross margin by segment, incremental ROIC
    • Competitive positioning using Porter's Five Forces (scored 1–5 per force)
- Variant View: identify the 1–2 consensus assumptions that are wrong, and provide
  your differentiated data or analysis to support your view
- Valuation Analysis:
    • Base case: DCF-implied intrinsic value; EV/EBITDA and P/FCF vs. comps
    • Bull case (30% probability): upside scenario with price target
    • Bear case (20% probability): downside scenario with floor valuation
    • Expected Value (probability-weighted) price target
- Catalysts: 3–5 specific, time-bound events that will close the gap between
  price and intrinsic value
- Risk Register: top 5 risks; for each — probability, magnitude, and how to
  monitor or hedge
- Position Sizing Recommendation: suggested % of portfolio with Kelly Criterion
  rationale
- Exit Criteria: what would make you close the position (thesis broken vs. price
  target reached)

Format as a Citadel-style Investment Committee Pitch Deck outline with:
  • One-Page Thesis Summary (Title Slide)
  • Variant View Evidence Slide
  • Valuation Football Field
  • Catalyst Timeline
  • Risk/Reward Summary Table

My inputs: [TICKER SYMBOL | LONG OR SHORT | SECTOR | CURRENT PRICE |
            YOUR KEY THESIS IN ONE SENTENCE | TIME HORIZON]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 12 — The JP Morgan Earnings Preview & Reaction Playbook
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Equity Research Analyst at JP Morgan Chase covering the technology
or consumer sector, responsible for writing institutional earnings preview notes
distributed to $500B+ in AUM across buy-side clients globally.

I need a comprehensive pre-earnings analysis and trading strategy for an upcoming
earnings announcement.

Deliver:
- Earnings History Scorecard: last 8 quarters — EPS beat/miss vs. consensus (in
  cents and %), revenue beat/miss, and stock price reaction on earnings day (%)
- Consensus Estimate Analysis: current Wall Street EPS and revenue consensus;
  identify the bull/bear range of estimates (highest vs. lowest sell-side estimate)
- The "Number That Matters": identify the 1 metric (beyond headline EPS) that will
  drive the stock on earnings day, with historical regression showing its correlation
  to post-earnings price move
- Segment Deep-Dive: analyze each revenue segment — expected growth, margins, and
  risks for this quarter
- Guidance Analysis: synthesize last quarter's guidance language; flag any
  overhangs or raised bars that could disappoint
- Options Market Intelligence: implied move (IV-based straddle cost), put/call skew,
  and what the options market is pricing as a 1-sigma move
- Scenario Analysis:
    • Beat & Raise: stock reaction estimate and key language to watch for
    • In-Line: likely muted reaction; identify what could still move the stock
    • Miss & Lower: downside estimate and support levels
- My Trade Recommendation: Buy Before / Sell Before / Wait / Use Options — with
  risk/reward justification and specific strike/expiry if options-based

Format as a JP Morgan Pre-Earnings Research Brief with:
  • Decision Summary Box at the top (one sentence recommendation + price target)
  • Earnings History Table
  • Scenario Analysis Matrix (EPS vs. Revenue beat/miss combinations → stock impact)
  • Options Strategy Diagram (described)

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | TICKER | EARNINGS DATE | CURRENT STOCK PRICE |
            YOUR POSITION (LONG / SHORT / NONE) | KEY CONCERN OR CONVICTION]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 13 — The KKR Leveraged Buyout (LBO) Model Builder
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Vice President in KKR's Private Equity group who builds and stress-tests
LBO models for the Investment Committee on deals ranging from $500M to $10B in
enterprise value.

I need a complete LBO model framework and returns analysis for a prospective
buyout transaction.

Build out the model:
- Sources & Uses of Funds: equity contribution, senior secured debt, subordinated
  debt / mezzanine, management rollover, seller financing — as % of EV and in $
- Leverage Structure: Debt/EBITDA at entry by tranche; interest rate assumptions
  (SOFR + spread); amortization schedule; covenant headroom analysis
- Operating Model (5-year projection):
    • Revenue: organic growth CAGR assumption + M&A bolt-on contribution
    • EBITDA margin: current → target through operational improvement levers
    • Capex: maintenance vs. growth; as % of revenue
    • Working Capital: cash conversion cycle improvement opportunity
    • Free Cash Flow available for debt repayment year-by-year
- Debt Paydown Waterfall: mandatory amortization + cash sweep; Debt/EBITDA at
  exit by tranche
- Exit Analysis (Year 4–6 sensitivity):
    • Exit EV/EBITDA multiple range: entry multiple ± 1.5x
    • Equity proceeds to sponsor at each exit scenario
    • IRR and MOIC sensitivity table (Debt/EBITDA at entry × Exit Multiple grid)
- Value Creation Attribution: returns from (a) EBITDA growth, (b) multiple expansion,
  (c) leverage / debt paydown — expressed as % contribution to total return
- Management Incentive Plan: typical PE equity rollover and options pool structure
- Key Covenant Tests: minimum EBITDA, maximum leverage, minimum interest coverage

Format as a KKR Investment Committee LBO Summary with:
  • Sources & Uses Table
  • 5-Year Operating Model
  • IRR / MOIC Sensitivity Matrix (primary output)
  • Value Creation Bridge (attribution waterfall)
  • Returns Summary at Base / Bull / Bear

My inputs: [TARGET COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM EBITDA | EBITDA GROWTH RATE |
            PROPOSED ENTRY EV/EBITDA MULTIPLE | TARGET HOLD PERIOD | DEAL SIZE]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 14 — The Sequoia Capital Venture Investment Thesis Builder
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a General Partner at Sequoia Capital who has led Series A through Series C
investments in enterprise SaaS, consumer tech, and deeptech companies that have
returned 20x+ to the fund.

I need a full venture investment thesis and deal memo for a startup I am evaluating.

Structure the deal memo:
- The Why Now: what market or technology inflection point makes this the right moment
  for this company to exist? Cite 3 macro or technology tailwinds.
- Market Sizing (Bottoms-Up): SAM → SOM in Year 3 → Year 5; validate with
  reference customers, ACV assumptions, and land-and-expand modeling
- Product & Technology Differentiation: rate on 3 axes — (1) technical moat depth,
  (2) time-to-replicate for a well-funded competitor, (3) proprietary data/network
  advantage
- Founder Assessment Framework: evaluate across (1) domain expertise, (2) ability
  to recruit, (3) capital efficiency mindset, (4) resilience/coachability
- Unit Economics Deep-Dive:
    • CAC by channel (paid, organic, sales-led)
    • Gross Margin %: current and target at scale
    • LTV using cohort retention data: Month 1, Month 6, Month 12, Month 24
    • LTV/CAC ratio and payback period — flag if < 3x or > 18 months
    • Magic Number / Sales Efficiency ratio
- Financial Projections: ARR bridge Year 1 → Year 3 → Year 5; Rule of 40 score
  at each stage; path to cash-flow breakeven
- Competitive Landscape: 3x3 competitive positioning matrix (axes: completeness of
  solution vs. ease of implementation)
- Valuation & Terms: pre-money valuation rationale vs. comparable Series A/B/C
  rounds; proposed term sheet structure (pro-rata rights, board seat, protective
  provisions)
- Fund Return Analysis: at proposed entry valuation, what exit value achieves 10x /
  20x / 30x for the fund? Is it plausible given comps?
- Investment Risks & Mitigants: top 5 risks ranked by probability × magnitude

Format as a Sequoia-style Deal Memo with:
  • One-Page Investment Summary
  • Market Sizing Bottoms-Up Model
  • Unit Economics Dashboard
  • Competitive Positioning Matrix
  • Fund Return Scenario Table

My inputs: [STARTUP NAME | INDUSTRY/VERTICAL | FUNDING STAGE | CURRENT ARR OR REVENUE |
            PROPOSED ROUND SIZE | PRE-MONEY VALUATION | TEAM BACKGROUND SUMMARY]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 15 — The Blackstone PE Portfolio Value Creation 100-Day Plan
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Operating Partner at Blackstone's Portfolio Operations group who
deploys cross-functional value creation teams into newly acquired companies to
accelerate EBITDA improvement within the hold period.

I need a 100-day Value Creation Plan (VCP) for a newly acquired portfolio company
designed to achieve measurable EBITDA improvement and position the company for
a premium exit multiple at Year 4–5.

Deliver:
- Value Creation Hypothesis: articulate the 3–5 core value creation levers specific
  to this company (e.g., revenue acceleration, margin improvement, M&A rollup,
  digital transformation, geographic expansion)
- Day 1–30 (Diagnose): assessment workplan for each lever; data requests; key
  management conversations; quick-win identification (must be actionable within
  90 days with < $500K investment)
- Day 31–60 (Design): detailed initiative design for top 3 value creation levers;
  business case per initiative (investment required, EBITDA impact, timeline,
  owner, KPIs)
- Day 61–100 (Deploy): launch plan for approved initiatives; management team
  augmentation plan; board reporting cadence and KPI dashboard
- EBITDA Improvement Bridge: from entry EBITDA → Year 2 → Year 4 target; show
  contribution by lever
- Management Incentive Alignment: proposed equity incentive plan with vesting
  tied to EBITDA milestones and exit proceeds
- Exit Multiple Expansion Strategy: what actions will justify a higher EV/EBITDA
  multiple at exit vs. entry? (Revenue quality improvement, M&A narrative,
  margin profile, ESG credentials)
- Risk Mitigation: top 3 value creation risks with contingency plans

Format as a Blackstone 100-Day Value Creation Playbook with:
  • Value Creation Hypothesis One-Pager
  • Initiative Prioritization Matrix (EBITDA Impact vs. Time to Realize)
  • EBITDA Bridge Chart (described)
  • 100-Day Milestone Tracker
  • Management KPI Dashboard Template

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | ENTRY EBITDA | ENTRY MULTIPLE |
            HOLD PERIOD | DEAL THESIS SUMMARY | BIGGEST OPERATIONAL CHALLENGE]

PROMPT 16 — The Apollo Credit & Distressed Debt Opportunity Analyzer
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director in Apollo Global Management's Credit & Distressed
Investments group, responsible for identifying and structuring investments in
stressed, distressed, and special-situation credits across the capital structure.

I need a full credit and distressed opportunity analysis for a company whose bonds
or loans are trading at a discount.

Analyze:
- Credit Quality Deep-Dive:
    • Leverage ratios: Debt/EBITDA, Net Debt/EBITDA, Debt/Equity at each tranche
    • Interest coverage: EBITDA/Interest, EBIT/Interest — covenant vs. actual
    • Liquidity runway: cash on hand + revolver availability vs. monthly cash burn
    • Near-term maturity wall: debt maturities in next 12 / 24 / 36 months
- Distress Catalyst Assessment: identify the 3 primary factors causing distress
  (operational, structural, liquidity, industry-wide) — rate severity 1–10
- Recovery Analysis:
    • Enterprise Value range in a downside liquidation scenario (use EV/EBITDA
      at distressed comps multiples)
    • Recovery waterfall by tranche: senior secured → second lien → unsecured →
      equity — show $ recovery and cents-on-dollar for each
    • Fulcrum Security Identification: which tranche controls the reorganization?
- Investment Opportunity:
    • Entry price and yield-to-maturity (or yield-to-worst) at current market price
    • Upside scenarios: (1) operational recovery, (2) out-of-court restructuring,
      (3) Chapter 11 plan of reorganization — model return for each
    • Downside: liquidation recovery for Apollo's proposed entry point
- Restructuring Strategy:
    • Recommended capital structure post-restructuring (target Debt/EBITDA)
    • Apollo's preferred entry vehicle (open-market purchase, DIP financing,
      credit bid, debt-for-equity exchange)
    • Key levers to create value post-restructuring

Format as an Apollo Credit Committee Memo with:
  • Credit Metrics Dashboard
  • Recovery Waterfall Table
  • Return Scenario Matrix (Entry Price × Recovery Scenario)
  • Recommended Trade/Investment with Position Sizing Rationale

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM EBITDA | TOTAL DEBT BY TRANCHE |
            CURRENT TRADING PRICE OF DEBT (% of par) | NATURE OF DISTRESS]

    • Categorize all spend into: Essential (must have), Value-Add (justify

      Sub-Activity → Line Item

  ZBB savings are real (cash in bank) vs. budget-only reductions

  resistance; top 5 cultural barriers and how to dismantle them
- Implementation Timeline: Pilot → Full Rollout → Embed in Culture
  (typically 18–24 months) with key milestones

PROMPT 23 — The Goldman Sachs Three-Statement Financial Model Builder
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Vice President in Goldman Sachs' Financial Institutions Group (FIG)
who builds and audits three-statement financial models for M&A, capital raises,
and management presentations.

I need a professional-grade, fully integrated three-statement financial model
(Income Statement, Balance Sheet, Cash Flow Statement) with dynamic scenarios.

Build the model architecture:

Income Statement:
- Revenue by segment with growth driver assumptions (volume × price × mix)
- COGS build: materials + labor + overhead with gross margin % check
- Operating expense waterfall: R&D, S&M, G&A as % of revenue with benchmark
- EBITDA, EBIT, EBT, Net Income, and Adjusted EPS reconciliation
- Share count: basic vs. diluted (treasury stock method for options/warrants)

Balance Sheet:
- Working capital build: DSO → Accounts Receivable, DIO → Inventory,
  DPO → Accounts Payable — fully linked to revenue and COGS
- PP&E rollforward: opening + capex − depreciation = closing
- Intangibles and goodwill rollforward (for companies with M&A history)
- Debt schedule: opening balance + draws − repayments = closing;
  interest expense linked to average balance × rate
- Retained earnings rollforward: net income − dividends = change
- Balancing check: Assets = Liabilities + Equity (flag if out of balance)

Cash Flow Statement (indirect method):
- Operating: net income + D&A + working capital changes
- Investing: capex, acquisitions, asset disposals
- Financing: debt issuance/repayment, equity issuance, dividends, buybacks
- Net change in cash; opening and closing cash check

Model Architecture:
- Color coding convention (inputs = blue, formulas = black, links = green)
- Scenario toggle: Base / Upside / Downside — all assumptions driven from
  a single Assumptions tab
- Sensitivity analysis: Revenue Growth × EBITDA Margin → EPS and FCF/share
- Output summary page: key metrics, ratios, and year-over-year bridges

Format as a Goldman Sachs Model-Build Specification Document with:
  • Model Architecture Diagram (tab structure described)
  • Assumptions Table (all key drivers with Base/Up/Down values)
  • Three-Statement Summary (year-by-year for 5 years)
  • Sensitivity Matrix
  • Model Audit Checklist (10 integrity checks)

PROMPT 37 — The Andreessen Horowitz (a16z) SaaS Metrics Deep-Dive
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a General Partner at Andreessen Horowitz (a16z) who has led Series B
and Series C investments in B2B SaaS companies, with a focus on product-led
growth, enterprise software, and AI-native applications.

I need a forensic SaaS metrics analysis to determine whether a startup's
unit economics and growth efficiency justify a premium valuation.

Analyze (using the "a16z SaaS Metrics Framework"):

Growth Metrics:
- ARR / MRR: current level, QoQ and YoY growth rate, and trend (accelerating
  or decelerating?)
- Net New ARR: new logo ARR + expansion ARR − churned ARR; analyze the mix
  and trend — what % of growth comes from expansion vs. new logos?
- ARR per FTE: measure capital efficiency of revenue generation; benchmark
  vs. comparable SaaS companies at same ARR stage

Retention & Expansion:
- Gross Dollar Retention (GDR): % of ARR retained from existing customers
  excluding expansion — flag if < 85% as a red flag
- Net Dollar Retention (NDR): including expansion; benchmark: good = 110%,
  great = 120%, exceptional = 130%+
- Logo Churn Rate: annual % of customers lost; acceptable thresholds by
  segment (SMB < 10%, Mid-Market < 7%, Enterprise < 5%)
- Cohort Analysis: plot Month-1 / Month-6 / Month-12 / Month-24 revenue
  retention for each annual cohort — are cohorts improving?

Sales Efficiency:
- CAC Payback Period: months to recover CAC from gross profit; target < 18
  months for SMB, < 24 months for Enterprise
- Magic Number: Net New ARR ÷ Prior Quarter S&M Spend; > 0.75 is efficient
- Sales Efficiency Ratio (LTV ÷ CAC): > 3x is the minimum; > 5x is strong
- Burn Multiple: Net Burn ÷ Net New ARR; < 1.0x is capital efficient

Margin & Path to Profitability:
- Gross Margin by ARR tier; benchmark 70%–80% for pure SaaS
- Operating margin trajectory; Rule of 40 score
- Burn rate and runway at current growth rate
- Path to FCF breakeven: at what ARR and with what headcount does the
  company reach FCF neutral?

Valuation Framework:
- EV/NTM ARR vs. public SaaS comps (segmented by growth rate and NDR)
- Premium/discount justification for the specific company
- NTM ARR estimate and implied EV at proposed valuation

Format as an a16z SaaS Metrics Investment Analysis with:
  • SaaS Metrics Dashboard (all key metrics vs. benchmarks)
  • Cohort Retention Chart (described)
  • Sales Efficiency Matrix
  • Valuation Benchmarking Table
  • Investment Verdict: Proceed / Pass / Re-evaluate at next data point

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | CURRENT ARR | GROWTH RATE | NDR | GDR | CAC |
            LTV | GROSS MARGIN | BURN RATE | PROPOSED VALUATION | ROUND SIZE]

  Copper, Lithium, and Gold — for each: current supply deficit or surplus,
  inventory levels vs. 5-year average, key supply risks (geopolitical,
  weather, mine depletion), and demand drivers
- Price Forecasts: 3-month, 12-month, and 3-year price targets for each
  commodity with the key assumptions and risks to the forecast

Energy Transition Investment Framework:
- "Green Metals" demand surge: quantify the demand increase for Copper,
  Lithium, Cobalt, Nickel, and Rare Earths from solar, wind, EVs, and
  grid storage — give tons per GW of installed capacity
- Supply response timeline: how long does it take to bring new mining
  supply online? Analyze the structural supply shortfall
- Investment opportunities: listed miners, streaming/royalty companies,
  and commodity ETFs that best capture the energy transition theme
  with valuation metrics and risk assessment for each

Inflation Hedging Portfolio:
- Historical analysis: which commodities have been most effective inflation
  hedges over the last 5 inflationary cycles (1970s, 1980s, 1990s, 2000s,
  2021–2023)?
- Portfolio hedge design: recommended allocation to commodities for a
  $500M portfolio seeking to reduce inflation sensitivity — specific
  instruments (physical, futures, ETFs, equity proxies), sizing, and
  correlation to portfolio's existing inflation exposure
- Risk considerations: commodity volatility, roll yield drag, contango
  vs. backwardation, and currency effects

Format as a Goldman Sachs Commodities Strategy Report with:
  • Super-Cycle Scorecard (Bull vs. Bear Evidence)
  • Supply/Demand Balance Table by Commodity
  • Energy Transition Demand Model (tons and $ by year)
  • Inflation Hedge Portfolio Allocation Recommendation

PROMPT 41 — The AlixPartners Operational Turnaround & Liquidity Stabilization Plan
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Managing Director at AlixPartners — the world's leading
turnaround and restructuring firm — who steps in as interim CFO or CRO
(Chief Restructuring Officer) at companies facing severe operational distress,
covenant violations, and liquidity crises.

I need a comprehensive 13-week cash flow stabilization plan and operational
turnaround roadmap for a company in financial distress.

Deliver:

Immediate Liquidity Crisis Response (Week 1–2):
- 13-Week Cash Flow Forecast (TWCF): build a week-by-week cash receipts
  and disbursements model; identify the minimum cash balance required to
  operate; calculate the liquidity runway and the specific week of cash
  exhaustion without intervention
- Cash Preservation Actions (immediate): payroll deferral feasibility,
  vendor payment stretching, capex freeze, discretionary spend elimination,
  asset sale candidates — quantify the cash impact of each
- Lender Communication Strategy: draft the Day-1 lender update message;
  how to present the crisis without triggering an acceleration; what to
  ask for (waiver, forbearance, amendment) and in what sequence

Operational Turnaround (30–90 Days):
- Revenue Triage: identify which revenue streams are cash-positive in the
  near term and which should be suspended to preserve cash; customer
  concentration risk if any customers are cut
- Cost Takeout Sprint: labor cost reduction options (layoffs, furloughs,
  salary reductions) with legal and severance cost analysis; supplier
  renegotiation playbook for top 20 vendors by spend
- Working Capital Release: AR acceleration (factoring, early payment
  discounts), inventory liquidation plan, AP extension program
- EBITDA Bridge to Stabilization: show the path from current negative
  EBITDA to EBITDA-positive within 90 days — which levers get you there?

Capital Structure Remediation (90 Days – 12 Months):
- Out-of-Court Options: amendment & extend, covenant waiver, debt
  exchange offer, equity injection from existing sponsors or new capital
- In-Court Options: Chapter 11 pre-pack vs. free-fall; estimated timeline,
  cost, and professional fee budget ($M); going-concern viability threshold
- Stakeholder Alignment: map each creditor class to their likely position
  (cooperative vs. adversarial); identify the fulcrum creditor

Format as an AlixPartners CRO Playbook with:
  • 13-Week Cash Flow Forecast (week-by-week table)
  • Liquidity Action Priority Matrix (Impact vs. Speed)
  • EBITDA Stabilization Bridge
  • Capital Structure Options Comparison (Out-of-Court vs. Chapter 11)
  • Stakeholder Alignment Map

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM REVENUE | LTM EBITDA (NEGATIVE OR LOW) |
            TOTAL DEBT | CASH ON HAND | NEXT DEBT MATURITY OR COVENANT TEST DATE |
            PRIMARY CAUSE OF DISTRESS]

PROMPT 42 — The FTI Consulting Chapter 11 Restructuring & Plan of Reorganization
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Managing Director at FTI Consulting's Economic & Financial
Consulting practice, serving as financial advisor to a creditors' committee
or debtor-in-possession (DIP) in a complex Chapter 11 bankruptcy proceeding.

I need a comprehensive Chapter 11 restructuring analysis and Plan of
Reorganization (POR) framework.

Structure the analysis:

Pre-Filing Assessment:
- Going-concern value vs. liquidation value analysis: build both scenarios;
  the difference is the "reorganization value" that justifies Chapter 11
  over Chapter 7 liquidation
- DIP Financing sizing: how much new money is required to fund operations
  through the Chapter 11 process? DIP facility terms (superpriority lien,
  roll-up of prepetition debt, milestones, fees)
- First-Day Motions priority: identify the critical orders needed on Day 1
  (cash collateral, DIP financing, critical vendor payments, employee wages)

Valuation & Plan Feasibility:
- Enterprise valuation for POR purposes using 3 methodologies:
  (1) DCF with court-appropriate WACC and terminal assumptions
  (2) Comparable company analysis with distressed peer adjustments
  (3) Precedent transaction analysis for similar Chapter 11 exits
- Reorganization value allocation: EV → secured debt recovery → unsecured
  recovery → equity value (if any); show cents-on-dollar by class
- New capital structure design: post-emergence debt capacity at 4–5x EBITDA;
  new equity distribution among creditor classes
- Feasibility test: can the reorganized company service its post-emergence
  debt and maintain adequate liquidity? Project 3 years post-emergence

Creditor Negotiation Framework:
- Absolute Priority Rule: who is in-the-money and out-of-the-money?
- Intercreditor disputes: identify likely objecting classes and their
  negotiating leverage
- Plan support agreement (PSA) strategy: which creditors to lock up first
  and how to structure the RSA (Restructuring Support Agreement)
- Timeline: filing → first-day orders → 341 meeting → disclosure statement
  approval → plan confirmation → emergence (typical 6–18 months)

Format as an FTI Chapter 11 Advisory Memorandum with:
  • Going-Concern vs. Liquidation Value Table
  • Reorganization Value Waterfall (by creditor class)
  • Post-Emergence Capital Structure & Feasibility Model
  • Creditor Class Recovery Analysis
  • Chapter 11 Process Timeline

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | LTM REVENUE | LTM EBITDA | DEBT STRUCTURE
            BY TRANCHE WITH AMOUNTS | PRIMARY REASON FOR FILING | KNOWN CREDITOR DISPUTES]

PROMPT 43 — The McKinsey Corporate Performance Transformation (CPT) Program
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Partner at McKinsey & Company's Transformation Practice who
leads large-scale corporate performance transformations for underperforming
companies that are not in financial distress but have chronically lagged
peers on ROIC, TSR, and organic growth.

I need a comprehensive corporate performance transformation program design
for a company that needs a step-change improvement in financial performance
without the urgency of a liquidity crisis.

Design the transformation:

Performance Diagnostic:
- Value creation gap: quantify the TSR underperformance vs. sector peers
  over 1, 3, and 5 years; decompose into: revenue growth gap, margin gap,
  multiple gap, and capital efficiency gap
- Root cause hypothesis tree: use a MECE issue tree to identify whether
  underperformance is caused by (a) strategy, (b) operations, (c) talent
  and organization, (d) capital allocation, or (e) external factors
- Peer benchmarking: rank the company on 10 key financial metrics vs.
  10 closest peers; identify the 3 metrics with the largest gaps

Transformation Architecture:
- Design the transformation across 4 horizons:
  H1 (0–6 months): Performance Baseline & Quick Wins — initiatives that
    generate EBITDA improvement within 6 months; no capital required
  H2 (6–18 months): Core Business Excellence — operational improvements,
    commercial productivity, and organizational effectiveness
  H3 (18–36 months): Strategic Repositioning — portfolio changes, M&A,
    new business models, digital transformation
  H4 (3–5 years): Sustained Outperformance — culture embedding, talent
    pipeline, continuous improvement operating system
- For each horizon: total EBITDA/TSR impact, investment required, key
  milestones, and required management capabilities

Execution Infrastructure:
- Transformation Office (TMO) design: governance, cadence, reporting
- Change management: the 7 elements of successful transformation
  (McKinsey's "Influence Model" applied)
- Performance management: new KPI framework tied to value creation;
  consequence management for underperforming leaders
- Talent strategy: who needs to be replaced, developed, or recruited
  to execute the transformation?

Format as a McKinsey CPT Program Launch Document with:
  • Value Creation Gap Waterfall (TSR decomposition)
  • Root Cause Hypothesis Tree
  • Transformation Initiative Portfolio (Horizon × EBITDA Impact × Investment)
  • TMO Governance Structure
  • 5-Year Value Creation Roadmap

My inputs: [COMPANY NAME | INDUSTRY | REVENUE | CURRENT EBITDA MARGIN |
            PEER MEDIAN EBITDA MARGIN | TSR VS SECTOR | PRIMARY PERFORMANCE CONCERN]

PROMPT 44 — The Macquarie Infrastructure Investment Underwriting Model
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director at Macquarie Asset Management's Infrastructure
division — the world's largest infrastructure asset manager — responsible
for underwriting greenfield and brownfield infrastructure investments in
energy, transportation, utilities, and digital infrastructure.

I need a comprehensive infrastructure investment underwriting analysis and
financial model for a specific infrastructure asset or project.

Underwrite the investment:

Asset Quality & Regulatory Framework:
- Asset type classification: (1) Regulated utility, (2) Long-term contracted,
  (3) Merchant/market-exposed — rate the revenue certainty and inflation
  linkage for each type
- Regulatory framework analysis: rate-of-return regulation, revenue cap
  regulation, or price cap? Key regulatory reset risk and RAB (Regulatory
  Asset Base) methodology
- Concession / contract terms: duration, revenue floor, volume guarantees,
  force majeure provisions, termination compensation

  assumptions with downside stress

  lifecycle maintenance capex schedule (major maintenance reserve)
- EBITDA margin: benchmark vs. comparable assets; operating leverage
- Capital structure: target Debt/EBITDA or Debt/RAB; typical infra
  financing: senior secured project finance, EIB/IFI debt, green bonds
- DSCR (Debt Service Coverage Ratio): minimum DSCR covenant; model
  DSCR under base and stress; identify the minimum revenue required
  to maintain DSCR ≥ 1.20x

  risk from energy transition, carbon pricing risk

Format as a Macquarie Infrastructure Investment Committee Paper with:

PROMPT 45 — The Blackstone Real Estate Private Equity (BREP) Acquisition Underwriter
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Vice President at Blackstone Real Estate Partners (BREP) —
the world's largest real estate private equity firm — who underwrites large
commercial real estate acquisitions across office, logistics, residential,
hospitality, and data center sectors.

I need a full real estate private equity underwriting analysis for a
commercial property acquisition.

Underwrite the deal:

Market & Asset Analysis:
- Market fundamentals: supply and demand dynamics in the target submarket;
  vacancy rate trend; rent growth trajectory (nominal and real); new supply
  pipeline as % of stock
- Asset quality assessment: physical condition score, location grade (A/B/C),
  tenant mix quality, WALT (Weighted Average Lease Term), and lease expiry
  profile
- ESG & sustainability positioning: LEED/BREEAM certification, energy
  intensity vs. benchmark, capex required to meet future sustainability
  standards (avoid stranded asset risk)

Financial Underwriting:
- In-Place NOI (Net Operating Income): Gross Revenue − Vacancy −
  OpEx − Management Fees − CapEx Reserve
- Going-In Cap Rate: In-Place NOI ÷ Purchase Price vs. market cap rates
- Value-Add Business Plan: identify the 3–5 initiatives to grow NOI
  (lease-up vacancy, mark-to-market rents, capex improvement program,
  operational efficiency) — with cost and NOI impact for each
- Exit Cap Rate assumption with sensitivity (±50bps) and rationale
- Waterfall Model: LP/GP structure with preferred return hurdle,
  catch-up, and carried interest split; calculate LP and GP returns
  in Base / Bull / Bear

Financing Structure:
- Senior mortgage: LTV (target 55–65%), DSCR, IO vs. amortizing,
  rate (fixed vs. floating + cap), and recourse provisions
- Mezzanine or preferred equity if applicable
- Total return metrics: Levered IRR, Equity Multiple, Cash-on-Cash Yield
  in Year 1, Year 3, and at exit

Scenario Analysis:
- Bear case: NOI 15% below base + exit cap rate 50bps wider → IRR impact
- Bull case: full lease-up achieved 12 months early + compression of exit
  cap rate 25bps → IRR upside
- Stress test: what rent decline % causes the loan to breach DSCR covenant?

Format as a BREP Investment Committee Underwriting Package with:
  • Asset Summary & Market Comparables Table
  • Financial Underwriting Model (Year-by-Year NOI, Debt Service, Cash Flow)
  • Waterfall Distribution Analysis
  • IRR Sensitivity Table (Entry Cap Rate × Exit Cap Rate)
  • Investment Recommendation

My inputs: [PROPERTY TYPE | LOCATION | PURCHASE PRICE | IN-PLACE NOI |
            VACANCY RATE | WALT | TARGET HOLD PERIOD | VALUE-ADD STRATEGY |
            TARGET LEVERED IRR]

PROMPT 51 — The JP Morgan Private Bank Single-Family Office (SFO) Design Blueprint
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

entrepreneurs and first-generation billionaires on establishing a Single
Family Office (SFO) after a liquidity event (IPO, acquisition, or secondary sale).

newly liquid family with $200M–$1B in net worth.

Design the SFO:

Make vs. Buy Decision:
- SFO vs. Multi-Family Office vs. Private Bank: compare on cost, control,
  privacy, customization, and talent for this specific wealth level;
  at what AUM does an SFO become cost-effective?
- Hybrid model option: outsourced CIO model with lean internal team

SFO Operating Model:
- Governance: Family Investment Committee structure, Investment Policy
  Statement (IPS), and family constitution / charter elements
- Staffing: recommended org chart with roles and responsibilities —
  CEO/COO, CIO (in-house vs. outsourced), CFO/Controller, Tax Director,
  Legal/General Counsel, Operations; fully-loaded compensation benchmarks
  for each role
- Technology Stack: portfolio accounting and reporting (Addepar, Archway,
  Black Diamond), tax/legal management, document management, cybersecurity
  — recommended vendors for each with cost estimates
- Annual Operating Budget: staff + technology + professional advisors
  (legal, tax, audit) + fund expenses; benchmark as % of AUM (typically
  0.5%–1.5% of AUM per year for an SFO)

Investment Management Framework:
- Core vs. satellite portfolio architecture: which strategies to manage
  internally (passive, systematic) vs. outsource to external managers

  managers; performance measurement vs. benchmarks; manager replacement

- Alternative investments access: direct co-investments alongside top PE
  firms; club deals; private credit; secondary market opportunities
- Liquidity management: maintain 12–18 months of family spending in
  highly liquid instruments; liquidity ladder design

Family Governance & Generational Wealth Transfer:
- Next-generation financial education program
- Philanthropic vehicle: Private Foundation vs. Donor-Advised Fund
  vs. Impact Investing — recommend based on the family's objectives

Format as a JP Morgan SFO Design Blueprint with:

PROMPT 52 — The Citi Private Bank Multi-Generational Wealth Preservation Strategy
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Design the strategy:

Wealth Preservation Philosophy:
- Define "success" for this family: wealth preservation in real terms
  (beat inflation), absolute growth, or transfer of specific assets?

  wealth erosion; the 3 root causes (investment risk, taxation, family

  purchasing power after: (a) inflation, (b) annual family spending, (c)

  illiquidity premium capture — recommended asset allocation with
  rationale for multigenerational time horizon

  short-term volatility can the family psychologically and financially absorb?

  and private equity — sizing each for inflation-hedging purposes
- Spending policy: sustainable withdrawal rate (endowment-style 5% rule
  vs. total-return approach); annual distribution framework

PROMPT 54 — The McKinsey Revenue Growth Management (RGM) & Pricing Strategy
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Partner at McKinsey & Company's Revenue Growth Management
practice who has delivered 5–15% revenue and margin uplift programs for
CPG, retail, and B2B industrial companies through disciplined pricing,
trade spend optimization, and portfolio management.

I need a comprehensive Revenue Growth Management strategy to maximize
revenue and gross margin without volume sacrifice.

Design the RGM program:

Pricing Architecture:
- Price-value mapping: plot each SKU/product on a price vs. perceived
  value matrix vs. competitive alternatives; identify under-priced and
  over-priced products
- Price elasticity analysis: segment customers by price sensitivity
  (must have / nice to have / commodity) and design differentiated
  pricing by segment
- Price architecture design: entry / core / premium tier strategy;
  price pack architecture (size and format mix optimization)
- Pricing rules engine: clear guidelines for when to give discounts,
  what approvals are required, and the floor below which sales cannot go

Promotional Effectiveness:
- Trade spend ROI analysis: for each promotion type (price promotion,
  display, feature) — measure revenue lift, baseline shift, and profit
  contribution; eliminate "negative ROI" promotions
- Customer P&L analysis: calculate the true profitability of each
  major customer after trade spend, logistics, and service costs
- Promotional calendar optimization: reduce non-working trade spend;
  redirect budget to highest-ROI activation types

Portfolio & Mix Management:
- SKU profitability analysis: identify the 20% of SKUs generating
  80% of profit; flag tail SKUs for rationalization
- Mix improvement strategy: trade customers up to higher-margin SKUs
  and formats; innovation pipeline focused on premium segment
- Channel mix optimization: compare gross margins by channel (e-commerce,
  mass, specialty, foodservice); recommend channel investment priorities

Revenue Realization & Leakage:
- Gross-to-net revenue waterfall: from list price → invoice price →
  net revenue after all trade, freight, and SG&A allocations
- Identify the top 5 revenue leakage points and the value at stake

Format as a McKinsey RGM Strategy Presentation with:
  • Price-Value Positioning Map (described)
  • Trade Spend ROI Analysis Table
  • Customer Profitability Ranking
  • SKU Rationalization Recommendation
  • RGM Value at Stake Summary (Revenue + Margin Impact by Initiative)

My inputs: [COMPANY REVENUE | INDUSTRY | CURRENT GROSS MARGIN |
            NUMBER OF SKUs | TRADE SPEND AS % OF REVENUE |
            BIGGEST PRICING CHALLENGE]

PROMPT 58 — The Accenture Strategy Corporate Venture Capital (CVC) Program Design
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director at Accenture Strategy who designs and launches

to accelerate innovation, gain early access to disruptive technologies,
and generate financial returns.

I need a comprehensive CVC program design for a corporate that wants to
invest in startups strategically.

Design the program:

CVC Strategic Rationale & Mandate:
- Strategic vs. Financial CVC: define the primary objective — is the
  CVC designed to: (a) access emerging technologies before competitors,
  (b) create future M&A pipeline, (c) generate financial returns, or
  (d) all three? Rank by priority and explain the trade-offs
- Investment thesis: define the 3–5 technology and market themes the CVC
  will focus on; explain why each is strategically critical to the parent

  a startup's strategic relevance to the parent on a 1–10 scale?

- Fund structure: evergreen corporate balance sheet fund vs. dedicated
  LP fund structure (advantages of each)

  relative to corporate revenue; investment pace (# deals per year);
  check size range (seed, Series A, B, C)

  CFO, external VC); decision-making speed (a common CVC failure point
  — how to move as fast as VCs?)
- Carried interest and incentive design for the CVC team: how to retain
  talent who could earn more at a pure-play VC firm?

- Stage allocation: % in seed/Series A (high risk/high reward) vs.
  Series B/C (lower risk, nearer to strategic use)

  to improve deal quality and signaling?
- Reserve ratio: how much capital to reserve for follow-on investments
  in portfolio winners?

Value Creation Beyond Capital:
- Strategic value-add playbook: how the parent corporation helps portfolio
  startups (customer introductions, distribution access, technology
  integration, talent) — and how to avoid the "corporate vampire" problem
  (taking without giving)
- Portfolio company engagement model: dedicated relationship manager per
  portfolio company; quarterly business reviews; M&A option framework

Format as an Accenture CVC Program Design Document with:

PROMPT 59 — The Perella Weinberg Partners Hostile Takeover Defense & Proxy Fight Advisor

PROMPT 60 — The Duff & Phelps Purchase Price Allocation (PPA) & Intangible Asset Valuation
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director at Duff & Phelps (now Kroll) — the leading independent
valuation firm — who performs Purchase Price Allocation (PPA) analyses for M&A
transactions under ASC 805 (US GAAP) and IFRS 3.

I need a comprehensive Purchase Price Allocation analysis for a completed
acquisition to properly allocate the purchase price to identifiable
intangible assets and determine the residual goodwill.

Deliver:

PPA Framework & Methodology:
- Explain the ASC 805 / IFRS 3 requirements: the acquirer must recognize
  all identifiable assets acquired and liabilities assumed at fair value
  on the acquisition date; goodwill is the residual
- Timeline: typical PPA must be finalized within 12 months of acquisition
  date ("measurement period")
- Required third-party specialists: valuation firm for intangibles, real
  estate appraiser for property, actuarial for pension obligations

Intangible Asset Identification & Valuation:
For each category, identify applicable intangibles and describe the
valuation methodology:

1. Customer-Related Intangibles:
   - Customer relationships: Multi-Period Excess Earnings Method (MPEEM)
   - Order / production backlog: Lost profits method
   - Key inputs: customer attrition rate, revenue per customer, EBIT margin,
     contributory asset charge (CAC)

2. Technology-Based Intangibles:
   - Developed technology / IP: Relief-from-Royalty (RFR) method
   - In-process R&D (IPR&D): Stage-of-completion adjusted MPEEM
   - Key inputs: royalty rate (Knobbe database benchmarks), technology
     life, obsolescence rate

3. Marketing-Related Intangibles:
   - Trade names and trademarks: Relief-from-Royalty method
   - Non-compete agreements: With/Without method

4. Contract-Based Intangibles:
   - Favorable/unfavorable contracts, licenses, franchise rights

Goodwill Analysis:
- Residual goodwill calculation: Purchase Price − Fair Value of Net
  Identifiable Assets = Goodwill
- Goodwill allocation to reporting units for impairment testing (ASC 350)
- Deferred tax impact: book vs. tax basis differences for each intangible
  create a deferred tax liability that increases goodwill

Fair Value of PP&E:
- Real property: sales comparison / income capitalization approach
- Machinery & equipment: replacement cost new less depreciation (RCNLD)

Format as a Duff & Phelps PPA Valuation Report Outline with:
  • Purchase Price Reconciliation Table
  • Intangible Asset Identification & Fair Value Summary
  • Goodwill Calculation Bridge
  • Key Assumptions and Sensitivity Analysis per Intangible
  • Deferred Tax Impact Summary

My inputs: [ACQUIRER | TARGET | PURCHASE PRICE | TARGET'S BOOK VALUE OF NET ASSETS |
            INDUSTRY | PRIMARY INTANGIBLE ASSET CATEGORIES | ERP PREMIUM USED]

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

PROMPT 61 — The Bridgewater Global Macro Portfolio Construction Engine
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Portfolio Strategist at Bridgewater Associates — the world's
largest hedge fund — applying Ray Dalio's "All Weather" and "Pure Alpha"
frameworks to construct a macro portfolio that performs across all economic
environments.

I need a rigorous global macro portfolio framework built on economic machine
principles, with explicit risk factor budgeting and scenario robustness.

Design the portfolio:

Economic Machine Framework:
- Decompose the global economy into its three core forces: (1) Long-term
  debt / productivity cycle, (2) Short-term debt / business cycle,
  (3) Political / geopolitical cycle — identify the current phase of each
  and the dominant market-moving force
- Risk factor identification: map all asset classes to 4 underlying
  economic environments (Rising Growth, Falling Growth, Rising Inflation,
  Falling Inflation); show historical returns for each asset class in
  each quadrant
- "All Weather" logic: construct a portfolio where the 4 economic
  environments are equally risk-weighted — calculate the risk contribution
  of each asset class using historical volatility and correlation

Risk Parity Portfolio Construction:
- Target portfolio: equal risk contribution from each economic quadrant;
  show the resulting asset weights (will be heavily bond-weighted vs.
  naive equal-weight)
- Leverage rationale: risk parity requires leverage to achieve equity-like
  returns at target volatility; calculate the required gross leverage and
  financing cost assumption
- Volatility targeting: set portfolio to 10% annualized volatility target;
  dynamic leverage adjustment mechanism

Pure Alpha Overlay (Active Macro Bets):
- Current macro views: translate the economic cycle diagnosis into 5
  specific macro trades with asset class, direction, sizing, and catalyst
- Carry trades: identify the highest carry opportunities adjusted for
  crash risk across rates, FX, and credit
- Correlation assumptions: which correlations are at risk of breaking down?
  (e.g., stock-bond correlation in stagflation)
- Stress scenarios: portfolio performance in 2008 GFC replay, 1970s
  stagflation replay, and 1994 bond market massacre

Format as a Bridgewater Macro Strategy Memorandum with:
  • Economic Quadrant Asset Return Table
  • Risk Parity Portfolio Weights (Current Positioning)
  • Active Macro Trade Book (5 trades with entry/exit/sizing)
  • Stress Test Results Table
  • Portfolio Risk Attribution (% risk from each factor)

            LEVERAGE CONSTRAINTS | GEOGRAPHIC FOCUS | BIGGEST MACRO CONVICTION]

1. Corporate Activism:
   - Target screening criteria: identify under-valued companies with

   - "Activist Thesis Construction": build the activist investment thesis

     opportunity, and design the specific asks
   - 13D filing strategy: ownership threshold triggers, public vs. private
     engagement sequencing, and coalition-building with other shareholders
   - Return attribution: how activist campaigns generate returns
     (multiple re-rating, earnings improvement, capital return, M&A)
   - Historical success rates: what % of Elliott campaigns achieve their
     stated objectives? What factors predict success?

2. Merger Arbitrage:
   - Spread analysis: cash deal spread (annualized) = (Deal Price −
     Current Price) ÷ Current Price × (365 ÷ Days to Close)
   - Risk assessment: deal break probability estimation; the 5 key
     break risks (regulatory, financing, MAC clause, shareholder vote,
     target performance)
   - Portfolio construction: optimal number of positions, position
     sizing based on deal break probability and spread, correlation
     between positions in a deal break scenario
   - Downside protection: where does the stock trade if the deal breaks?
     Estimate the "break price" for each position

3. Distressed / Special Situations:
   - Credit catalyst investing: stressed credits where a near-term event
     (refinancing, asset sale, covenant waiver) closes the price gap
   - Fulcrum security identification and distressed-to-control strategy
   - Post-reorganization equity: companies that emerged from Chapter 11
     with clean balance sheets and depressed valuations

4. Convertible Arbitrage:
   - Convertible bond valuation: parity value, conversion premium, credit
     spread, and theoretical floor
   - Delta-neutral hedge construction and gamma capture

Format as an Elliott-Style Event-Driven Strategy Framework with:
  • Activism Target Screening Scorecard
  • Merger Arbitrage Risk/Return Calculator Framework
  • Special Situations Opportunity Matrix
  • Portfolio Construction Guidelines
  • Scenario Analysis: Portfolio Return in Risk-Off Environment

My inputs: [PORTFOLIO SIZE | PRIMARY EVENT-DRIVEN SUB-STRATEGY FOCUS |
            RISK TOLERANCE | CONCENTRATION LIMITS | CURRENT OPPORTUNITY SET |
            TARGETED ANNUAL RETURN]

PROMPT 63 — The Two Sigma Quantitative Factor Strategy Design & Backtesting Framework
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Quantitative Researcher at Two Sigma — one of the world's most
sophisticated systematic hedge funds — who designs, tests, and implements
data-driven equity factor strategies with rigorous statistical validation.

I need a comprehensive quantitative factor strategy design and backtesting
framework to generate systematic alpha in equity markets.

Design the strategy:

Factor Universe & Selection:
- Factor taxonomy: classify factors into 5 categories:
  (1) Value: P/E, P/B, EV/EBITDA, FCF yield, dividend yield
  (2) Momentum: 12-1 month price momentum, earnings revision momentum,
      analyst estimate revision
  (3) Quality: ROE, ROIC, gross margin stability, earnings quality (accruals)
  (4) Low Volatility: realized vol, beta, idiosyncratic vol
  (5) Alternative Data: web traffic, satellite imagery, credit card spend,
      NLP sentiment from earnings call transcripts
- Factor construction: describe the exact calculation methodology for 5
  specific factors; importance of sector-neutralization and
  winsorization to avoid outlier contamination
- Factor decay analysis: at what holding period does each factor's
  predictive power decay? (Momentum: days/weeks; Value: months/years)

Backtesting Methodology:
- Point-in-time data: why using restated/revised data introduces look-ahead
  bias; how to source and validate point-in-time financial data
- Transaction cost modeling: realistic estimates for market impact,
  bid-ask spread, and borrow costs for short positions by market cap
- Portfolio construction: long/short portfolio with target factor exposure,
  risk model neutralization (beta, sector, country), and turnover constraints
- Statistical validation: t-statistic of alpha (need t > 2.0), information
  ratio, Sharpe ratio, maximum drawdown, and hit rate
- Overfitting avoidance: out-of-sample testing, walk-forward validation,
  multiple testing correction (Bonferroni / BHY), and factor decay
  monitoring in live trading

Multi-Factor Model Combination:
- Equal-weight vs. IC-weighted (Information Coefficient-weighted) factor
  combination; rank combination vs. z-score combination
- Factor correlation matrix: identify which factors diversify each other
  and which are redundant
- Regime-conditional factor performance: which factors work in bull vs.
  bear markets, high vs. low volatility regimes?

Format as a Two Sigma Quantitative Research Specification with:
  • Factor Construction Methodology Table
  • Backtesting Framework Checklist (15 quality checks)
  • Factor Performance Summary (IC, IR, Sharpe by factor)
  • Multi-Factor Model Combination Approach
  • Live Trading Implementation Checklist

My inputs: [TARGET UNIVERSE (US LARGE CAP / SMALL CAP / GLOBAL / EM) |
            TARGET HOLDING PERIOD | LONG/SHORT OR LONG-ONLY | AUM SIZE |
            AVAILABLE DATA SOURCES | PRIMARY FACTOR HYPOTHESIS]

PROMPT 64 — The Tiger Global Long/Short Equity Deep Research Process
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Senior Analyst at Tiger Global Management — the premier long/short

Research Process Design:

Industry & Business Model Analysis:
- Industry structure: Porter's Five Forces with quantitative scoring;
  market growth rate (TAM CAGR), cyclicality, capital intensity, and
  winner-take-all dynamics
- Business model quality rating: assess on 6 dimensions: (1) Revenue
  predictability/recurrence, (2) Pricing power, (3) Operating leverage,
  (4) Capital intensity, (5) Network effects, (6) Competitive moat durability

  through full lifetime; LTV/CAC by channel and customer segment;

  interview guide; questions to reveal switching costs and competitive
  vulnerability

  postings as a hiring/investment signal, patent filings

  price vs. volume; new vs. existing customers

- State the single most important thing the market is wrong about
  (the "Pivotal Question")

  the thesis within 12 months
- Quantify the variant view: if right, what does the stock do?

PROMPT 65 — The Goldman Sachs Bank Equity Research Fundamental Analysis
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Bank Financial Statement Framework:
- Income statement unique items: Net Interest Income (NII) = earning
  assets × NIM; Non-Interest Income (fees, trading, wealth management);
  Provision for Credit Losses (PCL) — explain the CECL methodology and
  how to forecast PCL through the credit cycle
- Balance sheet analysis: earning asset mix (loans, securities, cash);
  funding mix (deposits vs. wholesale); loan portfolio composition by
  type (C&I, CRE, residential mortgage, consumer) with credit quality metrics

  for top-quartile banks); identify drivers of efficiency ratio improvement

- Basel III/IV capital ratios: CET1, Tier 1, Total Capital — current
  levels vs. regulatory minimums and internal targets; stress capital
  buffer (SCB) implications
- DFAST / CCAR stress test: how the Fed stress test determines capital
  return capacity; estimate the maximum buyback + dividend under the
  current capital framework
- RWA optimization: explain Risk-Weighted Assets and how management
  can reduce RWA without reducing loans

- Price-to-Tangible Book Value (P/TBV): why TBV matters for banks;
  the ROTCE / (Ke − g) justified P/TBV formula; build a regression
  of P/TBV vs. ROTCE across the bank universe
- Dividend Discount Model (DDM): applicable to mature banks with
  stable payout ratios; sensitivity to ROTCE and payout ratio
- P/E and P/Pre-Provision Net Revenue (PPNR): through-the-cycle
  earnings normalization methodology
- Excess capital return analysis: how much capital above the target
  CET1 ratio can be returned over the next 3 years?

Scenario Analysis:
- Rate sensitivity: NII impact of +100bps / −100bps parallel shift;
  which loan and deposit categories are asset-sensitive vs. liability-
  sensitive?
- Credit cycle stress: PCL assumptions in a mild recession (NCO rate

Format as a Goldman Sachs Bank Research Initiation Report Framework with:
  • Key Financial Metrics Dashboard (NIM, ROTCE, CET1, Efficiency Ratio)

  • Rate Sensitivity & Credit Stress Scenario Table
  • Capital Return Capacity Analysis
  • Price Target Derivation with Methodology

- Define "additionality" — why the DFI investment must be different from
  pure private capital: (1) Markets DFI invests in are too risky for
  private investors, (2) Terms/structure commercial investors won't provide,
  (3) Demonstration effect that catalyzes subsequent private investment
- "Do No Harm" (DNH) and Environmental & Social (E&S) standards:
  IFC Performance Standards 1–8; which standards are most frequently
  violated in each sector; exclusion list (weapons, gambling, etc.)
- Additionality assessment: for a proposed transaction, prove that the
  investment would not happen without DFI participation

Financial Return Analysis for DFI Context:
- Return expectations: DFIs target risk-adjusted returns below pure PE
  funds (typically 8–12% net IRR for equity; 4–7% for debt); how to
  balance return and impact
- Risk-adjusted pricing: frontier market risk premium above EM; political
  risk insurance (MIGA) cost and coverage; credit enhancement tools
- Blended finance structure: first-loss tranche, concessional debt, and
  technical assistance grants — show how blended finance de-risks the
  transaction for commercial co-investors

Impact Measurement Framework:
- Theory of Change: map the intervention → outputs → outcomes → impact
  for the specific investment
- IRIS+ and HIPSO indicators: select the 5–10 most relevant impact metrics
  for the sector (e.g., for financial inclusion: number of new borrowers,
  % women borrowers, average loan size)
- Impact at entry vs. impact at exit: how to measure and report impact
  over the hold period; contribution vs. attribution challenge
- SDG alignment: map the investment to the specific SDG targets it advances;
  avoid SDG-washing

Frontier Market Due Diligence:
- Political and regulatory risk in frontier markets: the 5 risks unique
  to frontier investment (policy reversal, currency convertibility,
  force majeure, regulatory capture, land rights disputes)
- Governance and anti-corruption: FCPA/UK Bribery Act compliance;
  beneficial ownership verification; anti-money laundering assessment
- Exit strategy in illiquid markets: secondary PE sale, management buyout,
  strategic sale to regional company, local stock exchange listing

Format as a DFI Investment Committee Memorandum with:
  • Additionality Assessment Framework
  • Blended Finance Structure Diagram
  • Impact Measurement Framework (Theory of Change + IRIS+ Metrics)
  • Financial Return Analysis (Under DFI Return Expectations)
  • Frontier Market Risk Scorecard

My inputs: [SECTOR (ENERGY / FINANCIAL INCLUSION / AGRICULTURE / HEALTHCARE) |
            TARGET COUNTRY | INVESTMENT SIZE | PROPOSED STRUCTURE (EQUITY/DEBT) |
            DEVELOPMENT HYPOTHESIS | PRIVATE CO-INVESTOR IF ANY]

PROMPT 74 — The Citi Treasury & Trade Solutions Corporate Treasury Strategy
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Act as a Managing Director at Citi Treasury & Trade Solutions who advises

Design the treasury strategy:

Liquidity Management:
- Cash visibility: building a global cash pooling structure (notional
  pooling vs. zero-balance account sweeping); target jurisdictions for
  the master account; tax and regulatory considerations for cross-border
  cash concentration
- Liquidity reserve policy: define the minimum and target liquidity
  reserve (cash + undrawn revolver); stress test: 90-day cash flow
  shortfall under a severe stress scenario
- Cash investment policy: permitted instruments, credit quality limits,
  duration limits, and counterparty concentration limits for short-term
  cash investment
- Working capital optimization targets: DSO, DIO, DPO improvement
  through supply chain finance and dynamic discounting
